<a id="route:algorithm"></a>
#### The partition assigned to a word

Let $\mathcal A_G=\{w:\mathcal P_G(w)=\varnothing\}$ be the admissible domain. For $w\in\mathcal A_G$, compute $$\Phi_{\varnothing}(w)=(t,s),\qquad t\in T_{x_\mu},
 \qquad \Lambda_G(w)=\mu.$$ The finite algorithm on an individual input has four steps.

1.  Construct the triangular embedding and finite cone interval, and fix the common rational path representatives for every required $(H,y)$ before choosing any packet composition.

2.  For each required mask encountered, form its packets and evaluate $B_J$ through the local packet switches, common histories, and return to anchor subsets. Positive and negative packets use the two directions of the local switch, although the total jump has the positive sign.

3.  Evaluate $\Phi_{\varnothing}$ by the exact-mask recursion in Lemma <a href="#route:exact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="route:exact">39</a>, following its alternating walk until the output has exact empty mask.

4.  Read the multiplicities of the resulting weakly decreasing anchor word as the partition $\Lambda_G(w)$.

Nested calls strictly increase mask size, while each outer repetition follows an endpoint path in the finite union of two matchings. Thus the call depth is bounded and each alternating walk terminates. There is no need to enumerate the final fibers of $\Lambda_G$ or to supply the final elementary coefficients. Section <a href="#main:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:completion">7</a> gives the reversal for the original input convention. If the input is supplied in the insertion digits of Lemma <a href="#graph:digits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:digits">3</a>, first decode those digits by largest-vertex insertions. The construction then uses the resulting admissible word. Empty input is assigned the empty partition.

<a id="stmt-route-witness"></a>
<div id="route:witness" class="theorem">

<a id="route:witness-identity"></a>
**Theorem 40** (Permutation witness). *For every naturally labeled unit interval graph $G$, the preceding finite rule assigns a partition $\Lambda_G(w)\vdash n$ to each $w\in\mathcal A_G$ and satisfies the formal polynomial identity $$\label{route:witness-identity}
 X_G(X;q)=\sum_{w\in\mathcal A_G}
                   q^{\mathop{\mathrm{inv}}_G(w)}e_{\Lambda_G(w)}(X).$$ In particular, every elementary coefficient is in $\mathbb N[q]$ and has degree at most $|E(G)|$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-route-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-route-witness</summary>

*Proof.* For any padded partition $\mu$, exactly one word of multiplicities $(\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_D)$ has no strict ascent: the weakly decreasing word. This remains true when parts of $\mu$ repeat, since the anchor indices themselves are distinct labels. Consequently $\Phi_{\varnothing}$ identifies the individual words of shape $\mu$ with $T_{x_\mu}$. Energy preservation, Equation <a href="#route:word-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="route:word-energy">[route:word-energy]</a>, and Equation <a href="#route:history-series" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="route:history-series">[route:history-series]</a> give $$\sum_{\substack{w\in\mathcal A_G\\\Lambda_G(w)=\mu}}
       v^{2\mathop{\mathrm{inv}}_G(w)-M}
   =\sum_{t\in T_{x_\mu}}v^{E(t)}
   =[X_u]\Theta_{x_\mu}^{H_-}
   =[X_u]m_\mu(Y).$$ Multiply by $v^M$. The conversion $q=v^2$ is legitimate as a formal polynomial conversion by Proposition <a href="#route:parity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="route:parity">38</a>, including both its parity and its degree bounds. The coefficient identity of Proposition <a href="#graph:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:kernel">5</a> now identifies the right side with $[e_\mu]X_G(X;q)$. This proves the identity for each shape and hence Equation <a href="#route:witness-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="route:witness-identity">[route:witness-identity]</a>. The algorithms producing $\Lambda_G(w)$ use only elementary packets and local wall jumps; the coefficient identity certifies their output. ◻

</details>

<a id="main:completion"></a>
### The chromatic expansion

We return to the original graph and its graph-nondescent permutations.

<details id="proof-stmt-route-witness-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-route-witness</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>.* Choose the triangular embedding of Lemma <a href="#tri:embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tri:embedding">25</a>, and fix the rational generic choices and finite local orders in Section <a href="#route:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="route:section">6</a> by the enumeration specified there. Theorem <a href="#route:witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="route:witness">40</a> gives a terminating map $\Lambda_G:\mathcal A_G\to\{\lambda:\lambda\vdash n\}$ with $$\sum_{\substack{w\in\mathcal A_G\\\Lambda_G(w)=\lambda}}
       q^{\mathop{\mathrm{inv}}_G(w)}
   =[e_\lambda]X_G(X;q)=c_\lambda(q).$$ For $\sigma\in D_G^0$ define $$\theta_G(\sigma)=\Lambda_G(\operatorname{reverse}\sigma).$$ By Lemma <a href="#graph:digits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:digits">3</a>, reversal gives the required input to $\Lambda_G$ and complements graph inversions. Lemma <a href="#graph:reciprocity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="graph:reciprocity">7</a> then gives, for every $\lambda$, $$\sum_{\substack{\sigma\in D_G^0\\\theta_G(\sigma)=\lambda}}
       q^{\mathop{\mathrm{ginv}}_G(\sigma)}
   =q^M c_\lambda(q^{-1})=c_\lambda(q).$$ Together with $X_G=\chi_G$, this proves Equation <a href="#main:witness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:witness">[main:witness]</a>. Every coefficient is the generating polynomial of a finite set, so it belongs to $\mathbb N[q]$. All choices and all recursions are finite procedures for each input. For $n=0$, take the empty graph, the empty permutation and the empty partition. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 41* (Scope of the algorithm). The map uses the graph, the input permutation, finite wall recursions and local coefficient indexings. The elementary coefficient list is not supplied to it. The result proves termination, but gives no polynomial-time bound. The construction is a mathematical algorithm based on finite rational arithmetic and ordered finite sets.

</div>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The gap coefficients for $D = 3$. The horizontal order is time order, drawn schematically; phase order is $b_{2, 2}, b_{1, 1}, b_{1, 2}$. Vanishing bridge totals equate the two end coefficients on each level.",
      "figure_id": "tri:gap-figure",
      "pdf_page": 36,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "tri:gap-figure",
          "exact_quote": "The gap coefficients for $D=3$.  The horizontal order is time\norder, drawn schematically; phase order is\n$b_{2,2},b_{1,1},b_{1,2}$.  Vanishing bridge totals equate the two\nend coefficients on each level.",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.9cm,y=0.8cm,>=stealth]\n \\foreach \\x/\\name in {0/d_1,2/b_{1,1},4/d_2,6/b_{2,2},8/b_{1,2},10/d_3}\n   {\\fill (\\x,0) circle (1.6pt);\\node[above=5pt] at (\\x,0) {$\\name$};}\n \\draw[->] (0.12,0)--node[below=5pt] {$a_1$}(1.88,0);\n \\draw[->] (2.12,0)--node[below=5pt] {$a_1$}(3.88,0);\n \\draw[->] (4.12,0)--node[below=5pt] {$a_2$}(5.88,0);\n \\draw[->] (6.12,0)--node[below=5pt] {$u_{2,2}$}(7.88,0);\n \\draw[->] (8.12,0)--node[below=5pt] {$a_2$}(9.88,0);\n \\node at (2,-1.1) {level $1$};\n \\node at (7,-1.1) {level $2$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Two horizontal timelines with right-pointing arrows representing levels. Top timeline is labeled \"level 1\" below, containing points $d_1$, $b_{1,1}$, $d_2$ in order; the gap between $d_1$ and $b_{1,1}$ is labeled $a_1$, and the gap between $b_{1,1}$ and $d_2$ is labeled $a_1$. Bottom timeline is labeled \"level 2\" below, containing points $d_2$, $b_{2,2}$, $b_{1,2}$, $d_3$ in order; the gap between $d_2$ and $b_{2,2}$ is labeled $a_2$, between $b_{2,2}$ and $b_{1,2}$ is labeled $u_{2,2}$, and between $b_{1,2}$ and $d_3$ is labeled $a_2$."
    },
    {
      "caption": "The exact-mask algorithm follows a finite alternating path. An endpoint cannot enter a cycle in the union of two matchings.",
      "figure_id": "route:matching-figure",
      "pdf_page": 45,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "route:matching-figure",
          "exact_quote": "The exact-mask algorithm follows a finite alternating path.\nAn endpoint cannot enter a cycle in the union of two matchings.",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Latex,\n  vertex/.style={draw,rounded corners,inner sep=4pt,font=\\small}]\n \\node[vertex] (l0) at (0,1.3) {$w:\\ J$};\n \\node[vertex] (r1) at (2,0) {$(t_1,s_1):K_1$};\n \\node[vertex] (l1) at (4,1.3) {$w_1:K_1$};\n \\node[vertex] (r2) at (6,0) {$(t_2,s_2):K_2$};\n \\node[vertex] (l2) at (8,1.3) {$w_2:K_2$};\n \\node[vertex] (r0) at (10,0) {$(t,s):J$};\n \\draw[->,thick] (l0)--node[above,sloped,font=\\small] {$B_J$} (r1);\n \\draw[->,thick,dashed] (r1)--node[above,sloped,font=\\small]\n   {$\\Phi_{K_1}^{-1}$} (l1);\n \\draw[->,thick] (l1)--node[above,sloped,font=\\small] {$B_J$} (r2);\n \\draw[->,thick,dashed] (r2)--node[above,sloped,font=\\small]\n   {$\\Phi_{K_2}^{-1}$} (l2);\n \\draw[->,thick] (l2)--node[above,sloped,font=\\small] {$B_J$} (r0);\n \\node[font=\\small,align=center] at (5,-0.9)\n   {$K_i\\supsetneq J$ at every interior pair; endpoints have exact mask $J$.\\\\\n    Solid: required-mask matching.\\quad Dashed: larger exact-mask matching.};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Graph showing an alternating path between two rows of vertices depicted as rounded boxes. The top row has vertices labeled $w : J$, $w_1 : K_1$, and $w_2 : K_2$. The bottom row has vertices labeled $(t_1, s_1) : K_1$, $(t_2, s_2) : K_2$, and $(t, s) : J$. Solid arrows labeled $B_J$ go from $w : J$ down-right to $(t_1, s_1) : K_1$, from $w_1 : K_1$ down-right to $(t_2, s_2) : K_2$, and from $w_2 : K_2$ down-right to $(t, s) : J$. Dashed arrows labeled $\\Phi_{K_1}^{-1}$ and $\\Phi_{K_2}^{-1}$ go up-right from $(t_1, s_1) : K_1$ to $w_1 : K_1$ and from $(t_2, s_2) : K_2$ to $w_2 : K_2$, respectively. Text below states: \"$K_i \\supsetneq J$ at every interior pair; endpoints have exact mask $J$.\" Legend below states: \"Solid: required-mask matching. Dashed: larger exact-mask matching.\""
    }
  ],
